[1;38;5;78mDirection debate -
the rivals, and why
this one won[0m

[1;38;5;59mRival A - RECOVERY
ONLY[0m

Spend the whole wave
making wave 43's
four branches
independently
bulletproof and
merged, plus the
charter, and ship
nothing new. The
case for it is
strong and honest:
an unreviewed
authorization change
is the single
riskiest object in
the repo right now,
and a wave that
merges it carelessly
while chasing new
capability has
failed at the only
thing that mattered.
It was rejected
because it under-
uses the wave, not
because it is wrong:
four PRs of
independent re-
derivation is
roughly one round,
and the fold plus
the ladder are
DESIGNED work with
their obligations
already derived by
run - leaving them
unbuilt for a second
consecutive wave is
how wave 43's best
output rots. Rival
A's real
contribution
survives: movement 1
goes first, alone,
and no slice PR
merges on its own
author's oracle.

[1;38;5;59mRival B - ADVANCE
ONLY[0m

Treat the four
branches as
inherited fact,
merge them on their
green, and spend the
wave taking the door
from 3 of 19 to 6 or
7. Rejected
outright: it is
precisely the move
the wish forbids,
and the epic's own
law forbids it too.
A commit message is
an exit code with
better prose.
Merging #9332 on its
own test's word is
'a gate lies when
its green costs
nothing to produce',
committed by the
wave that coined the
sentence.

[1;38;5;59mRival C - THE DOOR
CENSUS (winner, as
movement 3 of three)[0m

The winning insight
is that the epic has
spent four waves
arguing about a
column it has never
computed. Every wave
restates the roster,
re-prices some rows,
and writes the total
into the charter by
hand - and PDS-D636
records that the
census itself
carries a FALSE
SENTENCE about its
own gating on main
today. That is the
exact failure mode
this epic exists to
end, committed by
the epic, about the
epic. Making the
column an instrument
is the epic's own
law turned inward
one more notch, and
it is cheap: the
derivation is two
greps over files
that already exist,
and the prior art is
already on main
(api/test/barkpark/p
ds_residue_lens_test
.exs).

[1;38;5;59mThe sharpest attack
on the winner, and
how the wave absorbs
it[0m

THE ATTACK: the door
census is a THIRD
census. This epic
already has a
receipt census, a
residue lens, a
ledger census, a
record-parity arm
and a charter-ledger
sweep - nineteen
instruments, three
of which run
anywhere. Building a
twentieth instrument
to count the
nineteen is the
purest possible
instance of an epic
measuring itself
instead of shipping.
And it will be WRONG
on day one: leg A
('a test file shells
it') has at least
three encodings in
the repo already
(System.cmd with a
module attribute,
Code.require_file, a
bare relative path),
so a naive
derivation will
under-count and
print a number LOWER
than the truth - a
different lie, in a
friendlier
direction.

HOW THE WAVE ABSORBS
IT. First, the door
census is movement
THREE of three - it
ships only if
movements 1 and 2
are honest, and the
wave is graded on
the security fix and
the merges
regardless. Second,
the under-count
objection becomes
the instrument's
design: the census
is FAIL-CLOSED on
ambiguity - a pds-*
script it cannot
dispose is
UNDISPOSED and reds,
and a test-side
reference it cannot
classify is an
ERROR, never a
silent 'not gated'.
That makes the day-
one wrongness LOUD
instead of quiet,
and the surveyors
are tasked (question
4) with enumerating
every encoding of
leg A that exists on
main before a
builder writes a
regex. Third, if the
survey shows the
derivation cannot be
made honest cheaply,
the fallback is
smaller and still
real: a self-check
that the two legs
AGREE for every
already-gated
instrument - which
alone would have
caught #9290 and
#9292.

[1;38;5;78mThe survey plan - 20
assignments, and
what each one
changes[0m

Width is the point.
Each surveyor is one
Opus at medium
effort; the digest
distils. Every load-
bearing assumption
in the direction
above appears below
as a question,
including the ones
that would kill it.

[38;5;59m• [0mPDS-D653 - A TASK
  CAN BE 11/12
  STAMPED AND ITS
  BRANCH EMPTY. pds-
  w44-door-census-
  instrument stamped
  eleven criteria
  with real run
  evidence, its now-
  line read
  'committing: ...
  branching loop-
  epic/the-price-
  column-stops-
  being-charter-pro-
  2', and git diff
  origin/main...that
  -branch is EMPTY.
  It was in NEITHER
  the built handoff
  nor the not-green
  list. The rule
  this buys: the
  LEDGER is the
  sweep index, not
  the handoff - diff
  every wave task's
  stated branch
  against
  origin/main and
  treat an empty
  diff as STRANDED,
  because a report
  cannot describe
  the builder that
  stopped before
  writing it.
[38;5;59m• [0mPDS-D654 - A GATE
  THAT DOES NOT
  TRIGGER ON ITS OWN
  SUBJECT IS GREEN
  FOR FREE. sheet-
  grid-js.yml runs a
  harness that
  parses
  root.html.heex,
  presence_state.ex
  and tokens_gen.ex,
  and triggered on
  NONE of them - so
  the contrast gate
  never ran on a PR
  that changed a
  hue, a background
  token, or the
  presence palette.
  D633's law in its
  cheapest form yet:
  no skip shim, no
  vacuous assertion,
  just a paths: list
  that omits the
  subject. Repaired
  in #9379.

[1;38;5;78mGrade - A-[0m

EARNED, not awarded.
What is genuinely
strong: three of
four slices closed a
defect that was REAL
and proved it by
substitution rather
than by reading, and
every one of them
refuted part of its
own brief from the
artifact - the
palette slice found
a THIRD stale
coupling nobody had
priced (--fg-muted
was matched for an
hsl() both themes
author as var(--
muted-text), so that
check had found zero
declarations and
could never have
run), and the census
slice refused the
ratio its dispatch
demanded. The
security slice is
the wave's best
work: it declined
the obvious fix
(widening
Caps.write_capable?/
2, which is shared
with the SheetGrid
prop and has no
target), declined
the obvious
predicate
(Access.admits_desk?
/3, whose
type/doc_id
overwrite IS the
bug), and carries a
positive control
proving a legitimate
grantee still
writes.

What falls short.
(1) A whole slice
died between its
last stamp and its
first commit and
NOTHING in the
wave's own reporting
said so - it took a
branch-vs-ledger
diff to find a
finished instrument
one cycle away from
deletion. That is
the sixth
consecutive wave to
strand built work,
and it is the single
reason this is not
an A. (2) Two
instruments shipped
contradictory prose
about the same
finding in the same
wave - a coherence
failure the wave had
every opportunity to
catch, since one
slice explicitly
refuted the other's
figure in its own
commit message. (3)
The security slice's
shipped non-vacuity
test is a local
constant-false STUB,
not the reverted
arm; the real revert
was run and recorded
in the stamp, but
the artifact that
MERGES cannot
reproduce it - the
reviewer had to re-
run the mutation by
hand to confirm. (4)
Coverage is honestly
partial and says so:
paper_ops/2 and
document_op/2 carry
the new arm but are
proven by READING,
and the census's
ExUnit rider was
never run locally.

No bloat: 489 lines
for the security
slice including a
361-line test, 333
for the census fold,
86 for the palette
repair. Nothing was
redesigned; the
reviewer's edits are
two latent-hazard
closures and one
prose
reconciliation.
Tree-tidiness was
not pursued and has
no value here.
